Paramount+

**Paramount+** is an American [subscription video on-demand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Video_on_demand#Subscription_models "Video on demand") [over-the-top](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Over-the-top_media_service "Over-the-top media service") [streaming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Streaming_media "Streaming media") service owned by [Paramount Skydance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Skydance "Paramount Skydance"). The service's content is drawn primarily from the [libraries of various properties owned by Paramount Skydance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_libraries_owned_by_Paramount_Global "List of libraries owned by Paramount Global"), such as [CBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS "CBS"), [Nickelodeon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nickelodeon "Nickelodeon"), [MTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV "MTV"), [Comedy Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comedy_Central "Comedy Central"), [BET](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BET "BET"), [The Smithsonian Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smithsonian_Channel "Smithsonian Channel"), [Showtime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Showtime_(TV_network) "Showtime (TV network)"), and [Paramount Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Pictures "Paramount Pictures"). It also shows original series and films, live streaming [sports coverage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broadcasting_of_sports_events "Broadcasting of sports events"), and in the United States, live streaming of [local CBS television stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_News_and_Stations "CBS News and Stations"). Paramount+ was originally launched on October 28, 2014, by [CBS Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_Corporation "CBS Corporation") as **CBS All Access** in the United States, initially focusing on the live streaming of CBS programming from its local affiliates, as well as on-demand access to CBS programs and library content. The service expanded into original programming in 2016. The service launched in Australia as **10 All Access** in 2018, taking its name from [Network 10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Network_10 "Network 10"), which was owned by CBS Corporation at the time, and consisted of a mixture between CBS and 10's programming. Paramount+ is one of the top ten [most-subscribed video on demand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_streaming_media_services#Streaming_video_on_demand "List of streaming media services") [streaming media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Streaming_media "Streaming media") service globally, with 79 million paid memberships.[\[3\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-Subscribers-19)

Following the [2019 merger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_merger_of_CBS_and_Viacom "2019 merger of CBS and Viacom") of CBS Corporation with [Viacom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viacom_(2005%E2%80%932019) "Viacom (2005–2019)"), content from Comedy Central, MTV, Nickelodeon, and other Viacom brands was subsequently included, and in 2021, the service was
renamed as Paramount+ on March 4, taking its name from Paramount Pictures, and was positioned as the flagship streaming venture of ViacomCBS, alongside [Pluto TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pluto_TV "Pluto TV") and [BET+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BET+ "BET+"). The service expanded into Australia, Latin America, and Europe.[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-ParamountPlus-20)[\[5\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-21)[\[6\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-22) Paramount+ is a sister service to [CBS News 24/7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_News_24/7 "CBS News 24/7") and [CBS Sports HQ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_Sports_HQ "CBS Sports HQ"), which are streaming services for CBS' [news](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_News "CBS News") and [sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_Sports "CBS Sports") divisions, [free ad-supported streaming television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_ad-supported_streaming_television "Free ad-supported streaming television") service [Pluto TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pluto_TV "Pluto TV"), which was acquired by Viacom nine months before completing its merger with CBS; and was also a sister service to [BET+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BET+ "BET+"), which was operated by BET and [Tyler Perry Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyler_Perry_Studios "Tyler Perry Studios"), before Paramount Skydance acquired Tyler Perry Studios' stake and merged it into Paramount+, and [Noggin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noggin_(brand)#Streaming_service_(original) "Noggin (brand)"), a streaming service that was launched by Viacom in 2015 which targeted preschoolers and featured content from [Nick Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Jr. "Nick Jr.") and Nickelodeon.

## History

### As CBS All Access

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/CBS_All_Access_Logo.svg/500px-CBS_All_Access_Logo.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:CBS_All_Access_Logo.svg) Logo as CBS All Access, used from October 28, 2014, to March 3, 2021

CBS All Access was launched on October 28, 2014, priced at US$5.99 per month with advertising and $9.99 per month without.[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-23)[\[8\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-NewWayToWatchCBS-24)[\[9\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-25)[\[10\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-26) Announced on October 16, 2014, as the first [over-the-top](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Over-the-top_media_service "Over-the-top media service") (OTT) offering by an American broadcast television network, the service initially encompassed the network's existing streaming portal at CBS.com and its mobile app for smartphones and tablet computers; CBS All Access became available on [Roku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roku "Roku") on April 7, 2015, and on [Chromecast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chromecast "Chromecast") on May 14, 2015.[\[11\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-27)[\[12\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-28) In addition to providing full-length episodes of past and present CBS programs, the service allows live programming streams of local CBS affiliates in 194 markets reaching 92% of the United States (including stations owned by [Sinclair Broadcast Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinclair_Broadcast_Group "Sinclair Broadcast Group"), [Hearst Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hearst_Television "Hearst Television"), [Tegna Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tegna_Inc. "Tegna Inc."), [Nexstar Media Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nexstar_Media_Group "Nexstar Media Group"), [Meredith Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meredith_Corporation "Meredith Corporation"), [Griffin Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Griffin_Media "Griffin Media"), [Gray Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gray_Television "Gray Television"), [Weigel Broadcasting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weigel_Broadcasting "Weigel Broadcasting"), and [Cox Media Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cox_Media_Group "Cox Media Group") and the launch group of [CBS Television Stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_News_and_Stations "CBS News and Stations")[\[13\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-29)), including [SEC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_Football_on_CBS_Sports "College Football on CBS Sports") sports and the [NFL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Football_League "National Football League"); however due to the absence of streaming rights, a few sports events are not streamed on the service (mainly involving [PGA Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGA_Tour "PGA Tour") events, some locally programmed NFL preseason games, and select [brokered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brokered_programming "Brokered programming") shows through _[CBS Sports Spectacular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_Sports_Spectacular "CBS Sports Spectacular")_), along with limited syndicated and [paid programming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infomercial "Infomercial") where only a local broadcast license to carry the program is allowed and web airing rights are retained by the syndicator or infomercial producer.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-30)[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-31)[\[16\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-32)[\[17\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-33)[\[18\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-34) By the very nature of its being live, streaming of a local affiliate does include all advertising, even with the commercial-free plan.

On December 1, 2016, CBS announced an agreement with the NFL to allow clearance of regional [NFL games carried by CBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NFL_on_CBS "NFL on CBS") on CBS All Access from Week 13 of the [2016 NFL season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_NFL_season "2016 NFL season") on. At the time, the games were blacked out on non– [Verizon Wireless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verizon_(mobile_network) "Verizon (mobile network)") mobile devices due to that provider's exclusivity agreement as the official wireless sponsor of the league.[\[19\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-35) In the [2018 NFL season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_NFL_season "2018 NFL season"), a new agreement with Verizon ending that exclusivity began to allow CBS All Access to stream games to all mobile devices; [Super Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl "Super Bowl") games run on CBS All Access without the need for any authentication.

As of February 2017,[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Paramount%2B&action=edit) the service had nearly 1.5 million subscribers.[\[20\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-Todd_Spangler-36) In August of the same year, CBS unveiled plans to expand CBS All Access to markets outside the United States. Canada was announced as the first international market to receive the service.[\[21\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-37) Plans to launch in Australia quickly followed, resulting from CBS's purchase of free-to-air broadcaster [Network 10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Network_10 "Network 10").[\[22\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-38)

In September 2017, _[Star Trek: Discovery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Trek:_Discovery "Star Trek: Discovery")_ premiered on CBS All Access, with its first episode also airing on the CBS broadcast network to promote the service. CBS reported that the premiere had driven its largest single-day increase in new subscribers since the [Grammy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/59th_Annual_Grammy_Awards "59th Annual Grammy Awards"). Along with _Star Trek_, [_Big Brother 19_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Brother_19_(American_season) "Big Brother 19 (American season)") and the start of the [2017 NFL season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_NFL_season "2017 NFL season") had also driven major increases in growth that month.[\[23\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-39)[\[24\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-auto-40)

Buoyed by _Star Trek: Discovery_, CBS All Access reached over 2 million subscribers by early 2018. The [60th Annual Grammy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/60th_Annual_Grammy_Awards "60th Annual Grammy Awards") also provided a boost to sign-ups, marking the second largest day for new subscriptions after the Discovery premiere.[\[25\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-TechC1-41) In April 2018, CBS All Access was made available outside the United States for the first time when it was launched in Canada.[\[26\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-CBSNews-2018-04-24-42)

The service launched in Australia in December 2018 as 10 All Access, named after Network 10. It operated alongside 10's free catch up and live streaming service 10 Play and contains a mixture of Network 10 and CBS programming. CBS shows are made available on All Access prior to being broadcast on 10's channels. 10 All Access is commercial-free and, unlike CBS All Access, has only one pricing tier.[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-43)[\[28\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-44)

In January 2019, CBS reported its largest increase in subscribers over a weekend—a 72% increase over the premiere of _Discovery_, crediting the premiere of season 2 of the series and that week's [AFC Championship Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Championship_Game "AFC Championship Game") (which also brought the service its largest streaming audience for a football game). [Super Bowl LIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LIII "Super Bowl LIII") would surpass this record only a few weeks later, with CBS reporting an 84% increase in new subscribers.[\[29\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-45)[\[30\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-46)

### Re-merger of CBS and Viacom

On November 25, 2019, as part of [the re-merger between CBS Corporation and Viacom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_merger_of_CBS_and_Viacom "2019 merger of CBS and Viacom"), CBS All Access announced the inclusion of programming from [Nickelodeon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nickelodeon "Nickelodeon"), as part of the wider launch of children's programming on the service, with other partners including [Boat Rocker Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boat_Rocker_Media "Boat Rocker Media") and [WildBrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WildBrain "WildBrain").[\[31\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-Nick-47)[\[32\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-48)

In January 2020, CBS All Access became available on the [Xfinity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xfinity "Xfinity") Flex platform, followed by the X1 platform in December.[\[33\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-49)[\[34\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-50)

On February 6, 2020, [CNBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNBC "CNBC") reported that [ViacomCBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Global "Paramount Global") was in discussions to launch a larger premium streaming offering, combining CBS All Access with content from [Paramount Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Pictures "Paramount Pictures"), the [Domestic Media Networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Media_Networks "Paramount Media Networks") division, and [Pluto TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pluto_TV "Pluto TV"). The service would include an ad-free tier, and a premium tier that includes [Showtime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Showtime_(TV_network) "Showtime (TV network)")'s streaming service. The company would maintain its existing streaming platforms, while marketing the new service to users of these other services.[\[35\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-51)[\[36\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-52) ViacomCBS partially outlined these plans in a corporate earnings call on February 20, 2020, stating that the expanded All Access service would take a "house of brands" approach to content and serve as a mid-tier offering complementing Pluto TV (which would remain a free service) and the Showtime OTT service by "adding a broad pay offering, built on All Access's foundation." The expanded service will include content from [MTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV "MTV"), [VH1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VH1 "VH1"), [Nickelodeon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nickelodeon "Nickelodeon"), [Comedy Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comedy_Central "Comedy Central"), [BET](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BET "BET"), and [Smithsonian Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smithsonian_Channel "Smithsonian Channel"), as well as a library of 30,000 television series episodes and up to 1,000 film titles from Paramount's film and television divisions and [CBS Media Ventures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_Media_Ventures "CBS Media Ventures") and expanded live news and sports offerings. No pricing plan or firm dates for content expansion were disclosed, though a "soft relaunch" will occur later in 2020. ViacomCBS will also continue to license its television and film content to competing streaming platforms.[\[37\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-53)[\[38\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-54)

On May 7, 2020, CBS All Access began adding more films to the service, starting with more than 100 from Paramount Pictures, and ViacomCBS announced that CBS All Access will expand internationally within twelve months.[\[39\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-55) On July 30, 2020, CBS All Access added several shows from [ViacomCBS Domestic Media Networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Media_Networks "Paramount Media Networks"), introduced a new user interface with "hubs" for different brands, and revealed that _[Kamp Koral: SpongeBob's Under Years](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamp_Koral:_SpongeBob's_Under_Years "Kamp Koral: SpongeBob's Under Years")_ (previously planned to air on Nickelodeon) would debut on the service in 2021.[\[40\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-Kamp_Koral-56) With the expansion, it was also announced that the service would rebrand in early-2021 to separate itself from CBS's platforms, and that there were plans to add multiple user profiles and parental controls later in 2020.[\[41\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-ViacomCBSAA-57)

### Rebrand as Paramount+

On September 15, 2020, it was announced that CBS All Access, along with 10 All Access would rebrand as **Paramount+** in 2021, and that it planned to perform more international expansion under the new name.[\[42\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-Rebrand-58)

On January 19, 2021, it was announced that Paramount+ would launch on March 4, 2021, with information being released on February 24, 2021, during an investor event.[\[43\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-59) The company announced that no updates will be provided for apps on second or third generation [Apple TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_TV_(device) "Apple TV (device)") once the service relaunches.[\[44\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-60) ViacomCBS announced during their investor event on February 24 that Paramount+ would premiere new 2021 theatrical releases from Paramount Pictures (such as _[A Quiet Place Part II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Quiet_Place_Part_II "A Quiet Place Part II")_ and _[Snake Eyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snake_Eyes_(2021_film) "Snake Eyes (2021 film)")_) 45 days after their theatrical release, _[PAW Patrol: The Movie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PAW_Patrol:_The_Movie "PAW Patrol: The Movie")_ and _[Clifford the Big Red Dog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clifford_the_Big_Red_Dog_(film) "Clifford the Big Red Dog (film)")_ received simultaneous theatrical and Paramount+ releases on August 20[\[45\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-61) and November 10, 2021[\[46\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-62) respectively, while other future theatrical releases from Paramount would premiere on the service either after their theatrical run or after their run on [Epix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epix "Epix") (which reached a new deal with ViacomCBS that same day to provide content for Paramount+, allowing recent releases from Paramount to be available on the service among other titles).[\[47\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-63)

The relaunch occurred as announced on March 4, 2021, with additional streaming content being launched and further rebranding efforts taking place at that time.[\[48\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-64) In August 2021, it was announced that Paramount+ would be shut down in [Nordic Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_countries "Nordic countries") in 2022 in favor of [SkyShowtime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SkyShowtime "SkyShowtime"), a joint venture with [Comcast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comcast "Comcast")-owned [Sky Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Group "Sky Group") that would also include content from Showtime, [Sky Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Studios "Sky Studios"), and [NBCUniversal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBCUniversal "NBCUniversal").[\[49\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-Nordics-65)

In September 2021, it was announced that Showtime's direct-to-consumer service would be offered as part of a bundle with Paramount+.[\[50\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-66) Showtime content would still be accessed via the Showtime app and website, but plans were announced for Showtime content to be accessible within the Paramount+ apps for its DTC subscribers later in 2022.[\[51\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-67)[\[52\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-68) The change was implemented in August 2022, with subscribers on both services able to upgrade to the combined "Paramount+ with Showtime" service for $11.99 per-month with ads and $14.99 without; a discount for new subscribers was available as an introductory offer through October 2.[\[53\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-69)[\[54\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-70)

On August 15, 2022, [Walmart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walmart "Walmart") reached an exclusive deal with Paramount+ to offer the streaming service as part of its Walmart+ offering. It meant that Walmart+ customers could access the ad-supported plan on Paramount+ at no additional charge.[\[55\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-71)[\[56\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-72) [Qantas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qantas "Qantas") also announced it had partnered with [Paramount ANZ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Networks_UK_&_Australia "Paramount Networks UK & Australia") to offer Paramount+ on its [in-flight entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In-flight_entertainment "In-flight entertainment") systems.[\[57\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-73)

On June 27, 2023, Showtime's direct-to-consumer service in the United States was fully integrated with the advertising-free premium tier of Paramount+, replacing the aforementioned bundle first introduced in 2021. The newly renamed "Paramount+ with Showtime" premium tier was raised from $9.99 to $11.99 per month while the "Essential" plan (containing advertising and no Showtime content) was raised from $4.99 to $5.99 per month.[\[58\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-74)[\[59\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-75) The Showtime Anytime app was discontinued on December 14, 2023.[\[60\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-76) That same month on December 6, Paramount announced that the apps for Nickelodeon, [Nick Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Jr. "Nick Jr."), MTV, Comedy Central, [Paramount Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Network "Paramount Network"), and Showtime would be discontinued soon. The apps were discontinued on January 31, 2024, although this did not include CBS' app.[\[61\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-77) The reason was to encourage more users to sign up for Paramount+. Paramount+’s sister streaming service [Noggin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noggin_(brand) "Noggin (brand)") was shut down on July 2, 2024 for the same reason. Noggin originally ceased operations as a television service on September 28, 2009, in favor of the [Nick Jr. Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Jr._Channel "Nick Jr. Channel").

### Merger of Skydance Media and Paramount Global

After some setbacks, [Paramount Global](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Global "Paramount Global") and [Skydance Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skydance_Media "Skydance Media") received [FCC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Communications_Commission "Federal Communications Commission") approval to [merge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merger_of_Skydance_Media_and_Paramount_Global "Merger of Skydance Media and Paramount Global") on July 24, 2025, which became effective on August 7 of that year.[\[62\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-78)[\[63\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-79) Prior to the creation of the new conglomerate, it was reported that [Oracle Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oracle_Corporation "Oracle Corporation") would help merge the operations of Paramount+ with its sister platform [Pluto TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pluto_TV "Pluto TV").[\[64\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-80) Following the formation of the new company legally known as [Paramount Skydance Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Skydance "Paramount Skydance"), a restructuring was created where Tom Ryan, president of Paramount Streaming (parent company of Paramount+), would report to Cindy Holland, president of the new Paramount Skydance Direct-to-consumer division.[\[65\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-81)[\[66\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-82)

On August 11, 2025, it was announced that Paramount had acquired the exclusive U.S. broadcast rights to the [UFC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UFC "UFC") (Ultimate Fighting Championship) for an estimated $7.7 billion over seven years. Under the deal, Paramount+ will become the exclusive streamer of all UFC events beginning in 2026, with CBS airing select events.[\[67\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-83)[\[68\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-84) On October 28, it was announced that Paramount had also acquired the exclusive Latin America broadcast rights to the UFC, as well as the rights for Fight Night events and preliminary cards for the mainline "numbered" events in Australia; the main card rights for these events are currently held by [Foxtel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foxtel "Foxtel") in the country.[\[69\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-85)

On August 13, 2025, [David Ellison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ellison "David Ellison") (CEO of Paramount Skydance) indicated that a "soft merger" of the company's two platforms (Paramount+ and Pluto TV), unifying their technological infrastructure but maintaining distinct [front-end](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Front_end_and_back_end "Front end and back end") identities, could occur within the next 12 to 18 months, meaning the merger would be completed by November 2026.[\[70\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-86)

### Proposed merger of Paramount Skydance and Warner Bros. Discovery

A few months following its formation, [Paramount Skydance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Skydance "Paramount Skydance") began making numerous bids to acquire [Warner Bros. Discovery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Bros._Discovery "Warner Bros. Discovery") (WBD). During the bidding process, it was reported that Paramount was interested in merging Paramount+ with WBD's flagship streaming service [HBO Max](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HBO_Max "HBO Max") to create a flagship streaming platform for the combined company.

Following a lengthy bidding war against [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix,_Inc. "Netflix, Inc."), Paramount increased its offer for WBD, prompting Netflix to exit the bidding war and allowing Paramount to proceed as the winning bidder.

Following the announcement of the merger, Paramount formally announced that it would merge Paramount+ and HBO Max into one streaming service, with which it aimed to reach more than 200 million streaming subscribers.

On April 23, 2026, WBD's shareholders approved the sale to Paramount Skydance.[\[71\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-87)

## Geographical expansion

In the United Kingdom and Ireland, the service launched on June 21, 2022, one day earlier than planned, and is available to watch on the Amazon Prime Video platform and as a standalone app.[\[72\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-88) Subscribers to [Sky Cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Cinema "Sky Cinema") received access to the service as part of their subscription, however in January 2025 the offering was shifted from the ad-free to the ad-supported subscription.[\[73\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-89)[\[74\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-90)

An announcement about the launch of Paramount+ in Italy, France, Germany, Switzerland and Austria in the second half of the year was made in early 2022.[\[75\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-91)[\[76\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-UKLaunchDate2-92) in late 2022, Paramount+ announced that the streaming service will be launched in France and French-speaking Switzerland on December 1, followed by its arrival in Germany, Austria, and German-speaking Switzerland on December 8.[\[77\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-GlobalExpend-93)[\[78\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-tdg.ch-94)

In September 2022, Paramount+ was replaced in [the Nordics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_countries "Nordic countries") by [SkyShowtime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SkyShowtime "SkyShowtime"), a joint venture with [Comcast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comcast "Comcast") that also includes [Sky Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Studios "Sky Studios") and [NBCUniversal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBCUniversal "NBCUniversal") content. SkyShowtime was subsequently launched throughout many other European countries over the course of late 2022 and early 2023, not including territories where Sky operates as a pay-TV provider or France.[\[49\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-Nordics-65)

The service launched in South Korea on June 16, 2022, on the [TVING](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TVING "TVING") video streaming platform operated by the entertainment company [CJ ENM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CJ_ENM "CJ ENM"). TVING subscribers could stream Paramount+ content at no additional charge,[\[79\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-UKLaunchDate-95) but the Paramount+ hub on TVING in South Korea ended after June 18, 2024.[\[80\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-SKShutDownDate-96)

In Japan, the service launched on November 15, 2023, on the on-demand platforms of cable provider JCOM and satellite broadcaster [Wowow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wowow "Wowow") at no additional cost to subscribers.[\[81\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-97) It later expanded as a paid add-on channel to Prime Video on April 17, 2024, and to [Lemino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lemino "Lemino"), operated by Japan's largest telecom [NTT Docomo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NTT_Docomo "NTT Docomo"), on April 18, 2025.[\[82\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-98) On February 18, 2026, it was announced that Paramount+ would cease all services in Japan on March 31 of the same year.[\[83\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-99)

In 2021, ViacomCBS issued a press release that revealed the combined number of subscribers to both CBS All Access and Showtime (another streaming product).[\[99\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-GodfatherFrasier-115) That press release revealed that the two streaming services had a combined 19.2 million US subscribers, though the release did not provide individual numbers for either service.[\[99\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-GodfatherFrasier-115) In May, ViacomCBS reported to have gained 6 million subscribers during the first quarter.[\[100\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-116) This brought the total number of global subscribers for ViacomCBS streaming platforms (Paramount+, Showtime, BET+) to 36 million. Out of the 36 million subscribers, a majority of these subscribers are subscribed to Paramount+; however, the exact number of subscribers were not released.

On February 15, 2022, Paramount announced that 32.8 million subscribers have signed up for Paramount+ as of the end of 2021. This number increased to 39.6 million as of late March and reached to 43.4 million by September.[\[101\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-117)

According to the _Wall Street Journal_, Paramount plans to raise subscription prices for Paramount+ in the United States in early 2026.[\[102\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-118)

## Programming

### Original programming

See also: [List of Paramount+ original programming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Paramount+_original_programming "List of Paramount+ original programming") and [List of Paramount+ original films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Paramount+_original_films "List of Paramount+ original films")

In November 2015, it was announced that CBS All Access would expand into original programming, announcing plans for a new _[Star Trek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Trek "Star Trek")_ television series produced by [Alex Kurtzman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Kurtzman "Alex Kurtzman") to premiere in 2017. The series would be the first television series in the franchise since _[Star Trek: Enterprise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Trek:_Enterprise "Star Trek: Enterprise")_ (which ran from 2001 to 2005 on former corporate sibling [UPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UPN "UPN")).[\[103\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-119) The series was later announced as _[Star Trek: Discovery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Trek:_Discovery "Star Trek: Discovery")_.[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-StarTrekDiscovery-120)

In May 2016, it was announced that _[The Good Wife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Good_Wife "The Good Wife")_ would get a [spin-off](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spin-off_(media) "Spin-off (media)") featuring [Christine Baranski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christine_Baranski "Christine Baranski")'s character [Diane Lockhart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diane_Lockhart "Diane Lockhart"); _[The Good Fight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Good_Fight "The Good Fight")_ launched with a CBS broadcast premiere in February 2017, with the remaining nine episodes exclusive to CBS All Access.[\[105\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-121) This became the first original drama on the platform, ahead of the delayed launch of _Star Trek: Discovery_ .[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-StarTrekDiscovery-120) In August 2016, it was announced that a streaming spin-off season of CBS reality series [_Big Brother_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Brother_(American_TV_series) "Big Brother (American TV series)") was being planned for CBS All Access.[\[106\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-122) The season, _[Big Brother: Over the Top](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Brother:_Over_the_Top "Big Brother: Over the Top"),_ premiered in September 2016.[\[107\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-THRFormat-123)

Further _Star Trek_ television series were announced for CBS All Access as well: in February 2019, CBS All Access announced what would become _[Star Trek: Picard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Trek:_Picard "Star Trek: Picard")_, focusing on the _[Star Trek: The Next Generation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Trek:_The_Next_Generation "Star Trek: The Next Generation")_ series' captain, which premiered in January 2020.[\[108\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-Title-124)[\[109\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-PremiereDate-125) The [adult animation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adult_animation "Adult animation") series _[Star Trek: Lower Decks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Trek:_Lower_Decks "Star Trek: Lower Decks")_ premiered in August 2020.[\[110\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-KurtzmanMar2019Updates-126)[\[111\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-127)[\[112\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-2020_Date-128)

With the service's rebranding as Paramount+, _[The SpongeBob Movie: Sponge on the Run](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_SpongeBob_Movie:_Sponge_on_the_Run "The SpongeBob Movie: Sponge on the Run")_ and spin-off series _[Kamp Koral: SpongeBob's Under Years](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamp_Koral:_SpongeBob's_Under_Years "Kamp Koral: SpongeBob's Under Years")_ debuted in March 2021. _Sponge on the Run_ was also made available to rent on demand on the same day.[\[113\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-129) ViacomCBS announced planned original series such as the [true crime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/True_crime "True crime") series _The Real [Criminal Minds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_Minds "Criminal Minds")_, a revival of _[Behind the Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Behind_the_Music "Behind the Music")_, a revival of the BET series _[The Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Game_(2006_TV_series) "The Game (2006 TV series)")_, [Taylor Sheridan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Sheridan "Taylor Sheridan")'s _Lioness_, and _[The Offer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Offer "The Offer")_—a drama based on [Albert S. Ruddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_S._Ruddy "Albert S. Ruddy")'s experiences filming _[The Godfather](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Godfather "The Godfather")_.[\[42\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-Rebrand-58) It later announced plans to produce continuations, revivals, and reboots of other properties, including _[Frasier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frasier "Frasier")_, _[iCarly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ICarly "ICarly")_, _[Reno 911!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reno_911! "Reno 911!")_, and a series adaptation of _[Flashdance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flashdance "Flashdance")_, among others.[\[114\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-130)[\[115\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-:000-131)

In February 2021, Paramount+ announced a [television adaptation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halo_(TV_series) "Halo (TV series)") of the [Xbox Game Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xbox_Game_Studios "Xbox Game Studios") franchise _[Halo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halo_(franchise) "Halo (franchise)")_ produced by [Amblin Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amblin_Television "Amblin Television") and Showtime, set to premiere in 2022. The show had been moved from Showtime's slate, as ViacomCBS positions the network as a premium "adult" service (as opposed to the broader positioning of Paramount+).[\[116\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-132)

In May 2021, CBS announced that _[Evil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evil_(American_TV_series) "Evil (American TV series)")_ and _[SEAL Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SEAL_Team_(TV_series) "SEAL Team (TV series)")_ would move from CBS to Paramount+ beginning with their second and fifth seasons, respectively.[\[117\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-CBSMove-133)

In August 2021, as part of the renewal of Comedy Central animated series _[South Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Park "South Park")_ through 2027, it was announced that series creators [Trey Parker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trey_Parker "Trey Parker") and [Matt Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Stone "Matt Stone") would produce 14 movie-length _South Park_ specials for Paramount+, with two premiering annually from 2021 through 2027. Streaming rights to the _South Park_ series proper are owned by [Max](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_(streaming_service) "Max (streaming service)") through 2025,[\[118\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-134)[\[119\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-135) after which streaming rights for new episodes will move to Paramount+ beginning in 2024 (season 27), and the series library in 2025.[\[120\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-136)

In May 2022, Paramount announced that Paramount+ was planned to commission 150 international originals by 2025, including several from Latin America (mainly Mexico) and European territories (including France, Italy, and Spain).[\[121\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-137)

In July 2025, Paramount announced that they had signed a $1.5bn deal with [South Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Park "South Park") creators [Trey Parker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trey_Parker "Trey Parker") and [Matt Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Stone "Matt Stone"), to bring to show exclusively to its streaming platform Paramount+ after a drawn-out bidding war with rivals including [HBO Max](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HBO_Max "HBO Max") and [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix "Netflix").[\[122\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-138)

### Sports programming

Paramount+ also carries sports programming, in conjunction with [CBS Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_Sports "CBS Sports"). In November 2019, CBS announced that it had acquired streaming rights in the United States to the [UEFA Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Champions_League "UEFA Champions League") and [Europa League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Europa_League "UEFA Europa League") in European soccer, replacing Turner Sports. All matches will stream on the service, with selected matches on the broadcast network and [CBS Sports Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_Sports_Network "CBS Sports Network").[\[123\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-139) The contract was initially to begin in the 2021–22 season and last through 2023–24. However, during a suspension of the [2019–20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_UEFA_Champions_League "2019–20 UEFA Champions League") season due to the [COVID-19 pandemic in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Europe "COVID-19 pandemic in Europe"), existing rightsholder [Turner Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Bros._Discovery_Sports "Warner Bros. Discovery Sports") opted out of its contract, and CBS took over the rights early for its remaining seasons.[\[124\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-140) On August 19, 2022, UEFA extended the deal until 2030.[\[125\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-141)

The service has picked up other domestic and international soccer events as well, including the [National Women's Soccer League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Women's_Soccer_League "National Women's Soccer League") (NWSL),[\[126\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-142) [CONCACAF Nations League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CONCACAF_Nations_League "CONCACAF Nations League"), [Women's Nations League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CONCACAF_W_Gold_Cup "CONCACAF W Gold Cup"), and [2022 CONCACAF Women's Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_CONCACAF_W_Championship "2022 CONCACAF W Championship"),[\[127\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-143) the [Argentine Primera División](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_Primera_Divisi%C3%B3n "Argentine Primera División"), [Brazilian Série A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campeonato_Brasileiro_S%C3%A9rie_A "Campeonato Brasileiro Série A"),[\[128\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-144) and Italy's [Serie A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serie_A "Serie A").[\[129\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-145) As part of the NFL's rights renewal with CBS, Paramount+ gains expanded rights to stream games on both its premium and ad-supported tiers between 2021 and 2033.[\[130\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-146)

On January 27, 2022, it was announced that the platform would broadcast all the matches of the [Chile national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chile_national_football_team "Chile national football team") as well as the rest of the [qualifying games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_(CONMEBOL) "2026 FIFA World Cup qualification (CONMEBOL)") (with the exception of the home games of the [Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazil_national_football_team "Brazil national football team") and [Bolivia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolivia_national_football_team "Bolivia national football team") national teams) towards the [2026 World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup "2026 FIFA World Cup"). which would begin in 2023, sharing broadcasts with [Pluto TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pluto_TV "Pluto TV") and the free-to-air television channel [Chilevisión](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chilevisi%C3%B3n "Chilevisión").[\[131\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-147)[\[132\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-148) Paramount+ has also acquired exclusive rights in [Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico "Mexico"), [Central America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_America "Central America"), [Belize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belize "Belize") and [Dominican Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominican_Republic "Dominican Republic") for the [Premier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premier_League "Premier League") starting in the 2022/2023 season,[\[133\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-149) and began carrying rights in Brazil to air [Copa Libertadores](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_Libertadores "Copa Libertadores") and [Copa Sudamericana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_Sudamericana "Copa Sudamericana") with the 2023 seasons for their respective football cups.[\[134\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-150)[\[135\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-151)

On May 22, 2025, it was announced that [World Rugby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Rugby "World Rugby") had secured an exclusive, multi-year, multi-platform rights agreement with CBS Sports for Paramount+ to become the U.S. home for all men's and women's World Rugby events through 2029, including the next three Rugby World Cups and all USA national team matches.[\[136\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-152)

### Syndicated and archived programming

The most recent episodes of CBS's shows are usually made available on CBS.com and Paramount+ the day after their original broadcast.

Paramount+ provides complete back catalogs of most of its current series, including full-season "stacking rights" (with the exception of certain series, such as _The Big Bang Theory_, which CBS held only "last five" episode rights during its original run, as [Warner Bros.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Bros. "Warner Bros.") retains all other rights as the show's distributor), as well as a wide selection of episodes of classic series from the [CBS Media Ventures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_Media_Ventures "CBS Media Ventures") program library – including shows previously owned by the original [Paramount Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Television "Paramount Television") made for both CBS and other networks prior to CBS's acquisition of its program library through the CBS-Viacom split (including the complete episode catalog of shows like _[Star Trek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Trek:_The_Original_Series "Star Trek: The Original Series")_, _[Cheers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheers "Cheers")_, _[MacGyver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MacGyver_(1985_TV_series) "MacGyver (1985 TV series)")_, _[Twin Peaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twin_Peaks "Twin Peaks")_, and _[CSI: Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSI:_Miami "CSI: Miami")_), along with the pre-1973 [NBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC "NBC") and [ABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Broadcasting_Company "American Broadcasting Company") libraries to subscribers of the service. Paramount+ also carries behind-the-scenes features from CBS programs and special events, and (beginning with the [17th season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Brother_17_(American_season) "Big Brother 17 (American season)") in June 2015) live feeds and special content from the reality series _Big Brother_.[\[8\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-NewWayToWatchCBS-24) However, with Warner Bros. licensing its content from its television and film library (even including a few HBO series) to Paramount+ in select regions like Australia, _The Big Bang Theory_ would soon be made available for streaming on Paramount+.[\[137\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-153)

In mid-April 2017, a limited library of films, made up of content from [Paramount Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Pictures "Paramount Pictures"), [Metro-Goldwyn-Mayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metro-Goldwyn-Mayer "Metro-Goldwyn-Mayer") (MGM), [Sony Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sony_Pictures "Sony Pictures"), [The Samuel Goldwyn Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Samuel_Goldwyn_Company "The Samuel Goldwyn Company"), and [CBS Films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_Films "CBS Films"), was made available on the service. This includes several titles in the [_Star Trek_ film series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Star_Trek_films "List of Star Trek films").[\[138\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-154) On May 7, 2020, CBS All Access added more than 100 films from Paramount Pictures;[\[139\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-155) more films were added in the following months.

In August 2019, CBS All Access expanded into children's broadcasting by acquiring the American broadcast rights to [_Danger Mouse_ (1981)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danger_Mouse_(1981_TV_series) "Danger Mouse (1981 TV series)") (as well as [the reboot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danger_Mouse_(2015_TV_series) "Danger Mouse (2015 TV series)") of the series), _[Cloudy with a Chance of Meatballs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cloudy_with_a_Chance_of_Meatballs_(TV_series) "Cloudy with a Chance of Meatballs (TV series)")_, and several other series from [WildBrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WildBrain "WildBrain") including _[Inspector Gadget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inspector_Gadget_(1983_TV_series) "Inspector Gadget (1983 TV series)")_ (and _[Inspector Gadget's Field Trip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inspector_Gadget's_Field_Trip "Inspector Gadget's Field Trip")_), _Bob the Builder_ ( [original](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_the_Builder "Bob the Builder"), and [reboot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_the_Builder_(2015_TV_series) "Bob the Builder (2015 TV series)")), _[Polly Pocket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polly_Pocket_(TV_series) "Polly Pocket (TV series)")_ and others.[\[140\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-156) On July 30, 2020, CBS All Access added 56 shows from [ViacomCBS Domestic Media Networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Media_Networks "Paramount Media Networks").[\[41\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-ViacomCBSAA-57)

On February 15, 2022, it was announced that Paramount+ would be the new streaming home for _[South Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Park "South Park")_ in the United States beginning with season 27 in 2024, after [HBO Max](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_(streaming_service) "Max (streaming service)")'s deal to the show will expire in 2025 in the United States and its separate international deal with [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix "Netflix") has also expired in 2022, with all current seasons of _South Park_ rolling out onto the service in countries where it is available, with new episodes premiering on Paramount+ in those regions.[\[141\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-157) This comes just seven months after the show's creators [Trey Parker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trey_Parker "Trey Parker") and [Matt Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Stone "Matt Stone") extending their deals with Paramount Global in August 2021; the streaming acquisition of the series will also include the rights to all 310 episodes beginning in 2022 in other countries and in 2025 in the United States.[\[142\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-158)

CBS's Christmas specials are not available on Paramount+.[\[143\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-159)

## Launch

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/Availability_of_Paramount%2B_in_the_World_%289_June_2025_update%29.png/960px-Availability_of_Paramount%2B_in_the_World_%289_June_2025_update%29.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Availability_of_Paramount+_in_the_World_(9_June_2025_update).png) Availability of Paramount+, as of 9 June 2025:

Available

Coming soon

Available in different website

Available in SkyShowtime

**CBS All Access** was first launched in the United States on October 27, 2014. The service would receive its first International expansion on April 23, 2018, when CBS All Access expanded to Canada. Australia received its own version of the service, named **[10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Network_10 "Network 10") All Access**, on December 4, 2018.

In August 2020, ViacomCBS announced plans to launch an expanded international streaming service using the CBS All Access technical architecture, but under the new **Paramount+** name (revealed in September) in 2021; the Paramount+ name would also be applied to the American replacement to CBS All Access. The service features original programming from CBS All Access as well as [Showtime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Showtime_(TV_network) "Showtime (TV network)"), plus additional programming including [Paramount Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Pictures "Paramount Pictures") films which may vary by market. The service would initially launch in the Nordics and Latin America, replacing an existing service of the same name, with additional markets to follow.[\[144\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-160) In Australia, while the relaunched service will premiere all new Showtime original programs going forward, currently airing series will remain on [Stan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stan_(service) "Stan (service)") until their conclusion under an existing deal.[\[145\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-161)

The Paramount+ brand itself was originated as a subscription [video on demand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Video_on_demand "Video on demand") film service, first launching in the Nordics in 2017,[\[146\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-162) and then in [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungary "Hungary"),[\[147\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-163) [Latin America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_America "Latin America"),[\[148\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-164) and [Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia "Russia") in the following three years.[\[149\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-165) The current iteration of Paramount+ in the Nordics and Latin America was preceded by this service.

Outside the United States, Paramount+ is currently available in Australia, Canada, Germany, Italy, Latin America, Middle East (as a pay TV channel), the United Kingdom and Ireland. Additionally, the Paramount+ SVOD service is still active in Russia (as **Okko Paramount+** and **IVI Paramount Play**),[\[150\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-166)[\[151\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-167) though the current streaming service is unavailable in said regions. Due to program rights and existing content deals, several programs are not available on local versions, or have delayed availability for new episodes.[\[152\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-TorStar-2018-04-23-168) For example, _[Star Trek: Discovery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Trek:_Discovery "Star Trek: Discovery")_ and all others from the franchise are licensed to [Bell Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell_Media "Bell Media") in Canada for their [CTV Sci-Fi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CTV_Sci-Fi_Channel "CTV Sci-Fi Channel") (in English) and [Z](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Z_(TV_channel) "Z (TV channel)") (in French) channels and streaming service [Crave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crave_(streaming_service) "Crave (streaming service)").[\[153\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-GlobeMail-2016-05-18-169)[\[152\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-TorStar-2018-04-23-168) As another example, selected Paramount+ original kids and family programming such as _[Kamp Koral: SpongeBob's Under Years](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamp_Koral:_SpongeBob's_Under_Years "Kamp Koral: SpongeBob's Under Years")_ and _[Rugrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rugrats_(2021_TV_series) "Rugrats (2021 TV series)")_ are licensed to [Corus Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corus_Entertainment "Corus Entertainment") for their [YTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YTV_(Canadian_TV_channel) "YTV (Canadian TV channel)") and [Treehouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treehouse_TV "Treehouse TV") networks and [Nick+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nickelodeon_(Canada) "Nickelodeon (Canada)") streaming service in 2021, in part due to pre-existing agreements between Corus and Paramount Global.[\[154\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-170) _[Star Trek: Prodigy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Trek:_Prodigy "Star Trek: Prodigy")_ is currently the only show of the genre not to be available on either Corus services or Paramount+. Instead, it airs in Canada on the CTV Sci-Fi Channel.[\[155\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-CTVSciFi-171)

A localized version of Paramount+ operates in the Middle East as a premium offering on pay-TV provider [OSN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OSN "OSN"), replacing the now-defunct [Paramount Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Network_(international) "Paramount Network (international)"). It features content from the aforementioned channel, as well as [Nickelodeon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nickelodeon_(Middle_Eastern_and_North_African_TV_channel) "Nickelodeon (Middle Eastern and North African TV channel)"), [Comedy Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comedy_Central_(Arab_TV_channel) "Comedy Central (Arab TV channel)") and [MTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_(Middle_Eastern_TV_channel) "MTV (Middle Eastern TV channel)").[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-172)

In India, Paramount+ original programming, along with Showtime and CBS programming were made available under Voot Select in [Viacom18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viacom18 "Viacom18")'s [Voot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voot_(OTT_Platform) "Voot (OTT Platform)"), a joint venture between [Paramount Global](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Global "Paramount Global") and [Network 18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Network18_Group "Network18 Group"), beginning in early 2021.[\[157\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-173)

In August 2021, [Comcast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comcast "Comcast") announced an agreement with [Paramount Global](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Global "Paramount Global") to launch SkyShowtime, a joint streaming service combining programming from the Paramount Global, [Sky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Group "Sky Group"), and [NBCUniversal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBCUniversal "NBCUniversal") libraries as well as original programming from [Peacock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peacock_(streaming_service) "Peacock (streaming service)") and Paramount+.[\[158\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-SkyShowtime-174)[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-175) The service is expected to be available in 20 smaller European territories, including four Nordic countries along with Hungary where it will replace Paramount+ and Poland where it will replace Paramount Play, instead of Paramount+ and Peacock operating separately in those markets.[\[49\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-Nordics-65)

In November 2021, it was announced that _[Star Trek: Discovery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Trek:_Discovery "Star Trek: Discovery")_ would be pulled from [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix "Netflix") in all countries outside the United States and Canada (for which [Bell Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell_Media "Bell Media") retained the license for the entire _[Star Trek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Trek "Star Trek")_ libraries) and moved to Paramount+ for international release.[\[160\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-176) Also reported were plans for its 2022 global rollout, starting with its launch in the United Kingdom, Ireland, Italy, Germany, German-speaking Switzerland and Austria via [Sky Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Group "Sky Group")[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-Sky-177) and South Korean entertainment and media conglomerate [CJ ENM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CJ_ENM "CJ ENM") to launch Paramount+ as a content hub on [TVING](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TVING "TVING") (similar to [Disney+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney+ "Disney+")'s [Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_(Disney+) "Star (Disney+)")), marking the first Asian region to launch the streaming platform; the partnership also includes a joint venture for future content, including English adaptations of [tvN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TvN_(South_Korean_TV_channel) "TvN (South Korean TV channel)") drama series which has produced by CJ's production house [Studio Dragon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Studio_Dragon "Studio Dragon").[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-CJParamount-178) During the earning call on May 3, 2022, it was announced that Paramount+ was set to be launched in the United Kingdom and Ireland on June 22, 2022,[\[163\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-pp-india-179) while the South Korean content hub launch was on June 16, 2022.[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-TVINGParamount-180) Paramount also announced that Paramount+ would launch in India from Viacom18 in 2023. It is yet to be announced on whether it will replace Voot along with [JioCinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JioCinema "JioCinema").[\[163\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-pp-india-179) The service as a content hub on TVING in South Korea was ended after two years in June 2024.[\[80\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-SKShutDownDate-96)

On February 15, 2022, during its annual investor presentation, French media conglomerate and cable operator [Canal+ Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groupe_Canal+ "Groupe Canal+") announced that Paramount+ will be launching in France in December of that year,[\[165\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-CanalParamount-181) followed by the announcement that the streamer will also expand into the [Caribbean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caribbean "Caribbean") by the end of the second quarter of that year.[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-182)

On March 28, 2022,[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-183) Caribbean cable operators [FLOW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flow_(brand) "Flow (brand)") and [BTC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BTC_(The_Bahamas) "BTC (The Bahamas)") announced that Paramount+ will be launching in the Caribbean via the cable companies' video on demand platforms.[\[168\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-FLOWPP-184) Within the region's subset covered by those VOD platforms, at least one such country was seemingly omitted from the initial rollout; however, some months later Jamaica was also added.[\[169\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-FLOWJamaicaPP1-185)[\[170\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-FLOWJamaicaPP2-186)

On November 27, 2022, the director of the Swiss branch of [Canal+ Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groupe_Canal+ "Groupe Canal+") announced that Paramount+ will be launching in French-speaking Switzerland on December 1, 2022.[\[78\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-tdg.ch-94)

Meanwhile, Paramount+ was released in other territories in Europe as SkyShowtime, beginning with replacing Paramount+ in the Nordics on September 20, 2022, launching in October 25 of same year, in the Netherlands and Portugal,[\[171\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-EuropeanDebut-187)[\[172\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-EuropeanStreamingLandscape-188) afterwards in the former [Yugoslav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yugoslavia "Yugoslavia") countries (except [North Macedonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Macedonia "North Macedonia")) on December 14 of that same year,[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-SkyShowtimeDecember14-189) and on February 14, 2023, in the rest of Central and Eastern Europe.[\[174\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-SkyShowtimeFebruary14-190) SkyShowtime finished its expansion by releasing in Spain and Andorra on February 28, 2023,[\[175\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-EuropeanRollOut-191) with its content later being incorporated as part of more expensive packages in the Spanish TV operator [Movistar Plus+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movistar_Plus+ "Movistar Plus+") at the beginning of 2024.[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-MovistarPlus-192)

Paramount+ had originally announced intentions of launching in [Southeast Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Asia "Southeast Asia"), [Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan "Taiwan"), [Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong "Hong Kong"), [Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Africa "Africa"), and the [MENA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_and_North_Africa "Middle East and North Africa") in 2023,[\[177\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-P+Global-193) but after the end of SkyShowtime's expansion in early 2023, the majority of these plans seem to have been scaled back or postponed, with Paramount Global opting to make Paramount+ available in new regions through partnerships with existing pay TV operators instead of expanding the reach of the standalone streaming platform.

In Belgium some titles from Paramount+ were made available exclusively on Streamz in 2022.[\[178\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-194) In November 2023, the deal with Paramount & Streamz was expanded so that a large part of the Paramount+ catalog would gradually become available on Streamz from December 22, 2023.[\[179\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-Streamz-195)[\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-Streamz1-196)

Paramount+ was launched in Japan on December 6, 2023, as an additional VOD hub for Japanese operators [J:COM](https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/JCOM "jp:JCOM") and [Wowow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wowow "Wowow") via their respective VOD services.[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-JapanLaunch-197)[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-JapanLaunch2-198) The hub was also made available on [Amazon Prime Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_Prime_Video "Amazon Prime Video") and [Lemino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lemino "Lemino") in 2024 and 2025 respectively.[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-199)[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-200) However, following an announcement on February 18, 2026, citing circumstances of the rights holder, the service officially ended across all four local platforms on March 31, 2026.[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-JapanDiscontinued-201) Similar plans were announced for Greece, in this case via the Greek cable operator [Cosmote TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cosmote_TV "Cosmote TV"),[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-GreeceLaunch-202)[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-CosmoteTVPlanned-203) with Paramount+ content becoming available on April 22, 2024, across the [Cosmote Cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cosmote_Cinema "Cosmote Cinema") channels and on demand.[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-CosmoteTVAvailable-204)

A deal was eventually signed for distribution of Paramount+ in the MENA with [beIN Media Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BeIN_Media_Group "BeIN Media Group") on October 22, 2024,[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-MENALaunch-205) with Paramount+ content now being present in the beIN's television channels, like [beIN Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BeIN_Series_(Middle_East_TV_channel) "BeIN Series (Middle East TV channel)"). This deal was also expanded into beIN's operations in [Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey "Turkey") on February 25, 2025.[\[190\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-TurkeyLaunch-206)

Additionally, Paramount+ was also announced for the Philippines as a content hub on [TAP DMV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TAP_Digital_Media_Ventures_Corporation "TAP Digital Media Ventures Corporation")'s OTT platform [Blast TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blast_TV "Blast TV") which was launched on May 23, 2025,[\[191\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-paramountplus-ph-207) and relaunched in South Korea under the new partnership with [Coupang Play](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coupang_Play "Coupang Play") in 2025.[\[192\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-KoreaRelaunch-208)[\[193\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-KoreaRelaunchDone-209)

On January 27, 2026, Paramount Skydance signed an exclusive content distribution agreement with New Zealand satellite television company [Sky Network Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_(New_Zealand) "Sky (New Zealand)") to distribute content from its assets including Paramount+ via its television channels and [Neon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neon_(service) "Neon (service)") streaming service.[\[194\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+#cite_note-210)
